Ant, Giant (Magical Animal, level 2)
Horace flicked the tiny ant off his arm, not thinking of it. It
must have talked. They can talk to their larger cousins. Not
long after, a yard long flying ant buzzed past us. We thought
little of it until the warriors attacked, spewing acid and rend-
ing with mandibles. They fought like mad, but the last two
fled. We'd lost one of ours, but decided to finish this. Foolish.
We tracked them back to the colony, crawled into the tight
tunnels. Strangely, we found little fight, clever traps our main
enemies. There were tunnels and chambers for what seemed
miles! Soon, I saw why they retreated: A queen was dying,
and the new queen was trying to escape with the aid of fly-
ers. We saw our chance, doused the area in oil, tossed a
torch, and never looked back.... -Harkis
Habits/Life Cycle: A colony consists of 200 to 4000 individuals.
The giant ants within a colony are all born of the same mother;
the queen.  She lays 6-12 eggs a day depending on need.  Each
egg takes two weeks to hatch and another week to grow to
maturity.  Each giant ant is born into its role in life.  There are
the workers, warriors, nurses, consorts and of course queens.  A
queen is born only when the current queen becomes ill.  She
actually suppresses the birth of a new queen until she is no
longer able to because of age or infirmity.  She can live 100
years but usually produces a new queen in her 75th or 80th year.
Individual giant ants live only 6 months and so must be replaced
constantly.  She regrows what is lost and expands the colony.
As it grows it becomes capable of feeding and defending higher
numbers.  Every giant ant in the colony has the compulsion to
defend her and will do so unto death.  giant ant colonies are
always in the ground.  The entrances are cleverly disguised as
rock face, tree stump or other artifice, with glue released by the
worker ants.  Inside the colony there are farms, nurseries, sleep-
ing areas, common grounds and somewhere, behind and below
the colony proper, is a maze of tunnels that leads to the queen,
designed with traps, guards and dead ends.  It is the last line of
defense.  When a new queen is born, there is instant strife in the
nest.  The first to defect are the nurses who hide and protect her,
followed by the workers and finally the consorts.  Only a small
group of warriors change sides and most will die alongside the
workers and the nurses who protect the new queen from the old
queens warriors, just as she and her consorts take her maiden
flight.  Those that survive this battle find their way to the old
queen and attempt to kill her.  If somehow they survive even
this, they are driven to search for the new queens colony.  It is
ironic, as the new queens minions do not recognize the sur-
vivors and quickly dispatch them.  Thus, the giant ants loyalty
is always repaid in death....
Climate/Habitat: Any but Highland, Icecap or Tundra.  Any
but ocean, exotic, mountains, rivers, tundra or polar.

Basilisk (Magical Animal, level 8)
An odd one (query: theres a normal giant, 8-legged magical
lizard?). Most creatures who paralyze use venom. This effi-
cient monstrosity has skipped the liquid component altogeth-
er, its gaze being sufficient not only to cause rigor, but to
turn the victim to stone. The gaze will be turned on all not
crafty enough to avoid it, until only a creature or two
remains, which the basilisk will then tear to pieces with teeth
and claws. It has a second transparent eyelid which covers
the eye and allows sight, but will block the gaze effect.
(query: Anti-basilisk goggles?)  Hypothesis: Created as a
guard beast? Its attack is very well thought out, and it is
asexual, needing no mate to perpetuate. Were it not for its
habit of making statues, Id say its a herd animal, as its hide
(either deep blue or iridescent green) and eyes, properly pre-
served, could prove a profitable set of products.... -Turgis
Habits/Life Cycle: These creatures like quiet, out of the way
places, where they can surprise their victims and petrify them.
The Basilisk can reproduce without a mate once every 5 years,
then it lays 2-4 eggs.  It prefers to lay them in dark, warm places
so that it does not have to incubate them.  It can however lay
curled around them if the temperature is too low to otherwise be
safe.  It has an instinct to protect the eggs and will do so for the
3-4 weeks it takes them to hatch.  The Basilisk leaves them just
before they hatch.  Basilisk eggs hatch one at a time about every
four hours.  Their formidable petrifying gaze manifests three
months after the time they hatch.  An egg that does not get
enough heat turns to gray granite-like stone.  Some of these
become dormant and can remain so for years until the proper
heat source warms them.  When the egg is finally exposed to a
heat source, it turns back into its natural form and hatches 3-4
weeks later.  The Basilisk can live as long as 90-100 years.
Climate/Habitat: Humid Continental, Humid Oceanic, Tropical
Wet, and Humid Subtropical.   Rain forests, jungles, forests,
marsh, swamp and subterranean (near surface and deep).
Body Type Huge (quadruped with 4 additional legs, shins and
claws)
STR 13, AGL 15, VIT 13, MEN 2, PER 2, MYS 12
MR 60%, CR 40%, IR 0%, PR 40%, DR 35% 
END 90, Hide 30 (1 per 3 End), Shock 270 
Melee +0, Missile +0, Damage +8, 
Defensive -1, Initiative -4 
Height/Length 7 Weight 750 lb. 
Movement
Walk 11/110/11, Trot 20/200/20, Run 30/300/30
Attacks (Intermediate Attack Skill)  
Bite (4 seconds, 6d6 cutting)
Claw (3 seconds, 5d6 cutting)
Raking Claws (3 seconds, 6d6 cutting, used while grappling)
Gaze Effect (Transmutes to granite)
Attack Notes: The Basilisk wriggles about to catch the gaze of
its opponents and convert them to stone. The Basilisk will
attempt transforming all but one victim to stone after this it will
try to finish its final foes with claw and bite.
Experience Value: 10400 
Skills: Climb 80%, Swimming 60%, Force 40%.
Abilities: Additional Limbs, Hide (scales), Heat Sensing,
Enhanced Smell, Transmuting Gaze, Enhanced Magic
Resistance, Medium Fangs.
Special Notes  
Detect Magic: The Basilisk radiates magic.
Tame Animal: The Basilisk is untameable.  
Taxidermy: If the Basilisks eyes are preserved in brine,
formaldehyde or some other substance, the gaze effect will
remain with them for about a year. 
Basilisk, Red (Magical Animal, level 8)
It is hard to say which breed came first. The red basilisk is
just as deadly as its non-crimson cousin as well as having an
immunity to fire. Reports it can spit flame are unsubstanti-
ated,  but what it does trade for being able to walk on coals
(query: Can the meat be cooked? What does basilisk tartar
taste like?) is a vulnerability to its own gaze, which might
make mating complex, were it not asexual. The specimen I
examined bore no inner eyelids, so the reds must be more
careful about turning its last victim to stone, else inadver-
tently starve to death.  Unable to be tamed by any known
means, it is definitely not a lap pet, and has a preference for
very hot places, making it easy to avoid.  It is a shame, as it
turns its victims to volcanic stone, which is so much more
attractive than the granite its cousins produce. -Turgis
Habits/Life Cycle: The Red Basilisk will only lay its clutch of
eggs on a very hot surface, such as burning sands or a recent
lava flow.  Each Red Basilisk is asexual and lays a single clutch
of 7-12 eggs just before dying.  A proper heat source to incubate
the eggs will result in the hatchlings bursting forth 2-4 days
later.  They will devour the parents carcass, or each other, if no
other immediate food source exists.  Their dreaded gaze ability
does not manifest until three months after the time they hatch.
If no sufficient heat source exists to incubate the eggs, the
unborn hatchlings will perish, turning the egg a dark black color,
resembling obsidian.  Occasionally, a dark black egg will enter a
state of hibernation instead of dying, regaining its red color and
hatching once a strong heat source is present.  The Red Basilisk
lives about 75-80 years.
Climate/Habitat: Underground and mountains near active volca-

Bat (Animal, level 0)
Note:  Curse it, they can see. The old adages are wrong.
What would my grandmother say? Argh. Well, they may be a
rare flying mammals, and useful for scooping up bugs and
capable of navigating by sound, but that gives them no
excuse for making a fool of my grandmother. Wonder how
hard it would be to blind them all.... -Turgis
Habits/Life Cycle: Bats roost in caves, hollowed out trees,
foliage in trees, cracks in bark and even in rocks.  Some have
adhesive on their ankles and wrists.  Varieties that live in caves,
trees, or ruins will form huge colonies.  Some colonies can con-
tain literally millions of bats.  Yet the bats low rates of repro-
duction keeps the species from overpopulating in most areas.
Bats are born live and require 8 months of gestation on average.
A bat can live anywhere from two years to thirty, but ten is the
most common.
Climate/Habitat: Desert, Semiarid, humid continental, humid
subtropical, subtropical Dry Summer, tropical wet and tropical
wet and Dry. desert, forest, rain forest, scrublands, subterranean
(near surface) and are also found in dwellings or ruins.

Bee, Giant (Magical Animal, level 2)
Our sergeant was speaking to the fairy elder, which was a
cue for us to light our pipes. I sat down, stretched, and there
it was: two feet long, yellow and black... and with a saddle. A
fairy was sitting on its back, pipe in hand, which he offered.
After a little tobacco, we got to talking, and I asked about
his mount. He said, "We can relate. It's like how I love my
queen. Imagine, thousands of servants, devoted only to you
and your realm. It's kind of romantic." I asked if he ever
feared the sting, and he said, Only that if my bee ever used
his, itd pull his guts out. The other guy 'd be done for, but
he'd die too." He said that the proboscis was worse, meant
to pull sap from hardwood or bore out a tree to get building
blocks for the hive.... -Harkis
Habits/Life Cycle: A typical hive is a group of 30 to 120 indi-
viduals and one queen.  Of the individuals besides the queen
there are two varieties, workers and drones.  Drones are only
produced when there is plenty of available sap and the season is
favorable.  Queens are born when rich, royal jelly is supplied by
the nurse workers.  The queen determines which eggs are born,
killing or eating rival queens and unwanted drones.  Once in 5
years, if the hive is thriving, she will allow another queen to be
born.  The hive then divides in twain without struggle or contest,
half leaving with the old queen to search for a new home and
half remaining to nurture and serve the new queen.  The nest
usually covers an entire mature oak; it lasts no more than two to
three years before breaking the tree from the strain of its weight.
When the hive is near failing, the bees abandon it and move on.
Giant bees speak to each other through elaborate dances.  These
dances describe direction and distance of sap rich trees and ene-
mies.  Every giant bee lives 2 years, save for the queen who can
live from 25 to 30 years.  
Climate/Habitat: Any but Highland, Icecap or Tundra.  Any but
ocean, exotic, mountains, rivers, tundra or polar.

Centipede, Giant
(Magical Animal, level 7)
Nothing should have that many legs. Nothing should move like
that. Nothing should have those mandibles or subtle poison.
Why did some god decide to get rid of the only thing that
keeps us from really fearing bugs away by making them big?
Did some mage get careless and bored? Didn't they know?
And why the hell did it crawl into my tent? -Harkis
Habits/Life Cycle:  The female lays 6 eggs in rotted logs by
the dozens in spring time.  If a male is nearby it will be attracted
to the odor and will fertilize the eggs by instinct.  The eggs
hatch and they will consume the log, each other and anything
they come in contact with the rest of their lives, which can be as
long as thirty or more years.  They do not reach maturity but
continue growing as long as food is abundant until their internal
organs finally fail.  They often hollow out small complexes near
the surface in which they lay when surface conditions are not
favorable.

in Humid Oceanic and Humid Continental

Deer (Animal, level 1)
Oh the life of a deer! Beset by predators on four legs or
two, skittish, fearful, but still so graceful and lovely as to be

them as fur and meat.... -Pock
Habits/Life Cycle: Born alive these animals reach maturity in

reindeer of either sex) grows antlers and then loses them again
each spring; they only grow to nubs in the first season, but in
seasons that follow they grow larger, with more points indicat-
ing a long life and a healthy male.

Drake, Fire (Magical Animal, level 7)
The fool said he was going to bring me a dragon. These
morons. Cant they tell a sick drake from a creature that all
men fear? Surely, drakes are fearsome enough, with claw and
ability to spit fire. Just look at the atrophied wings, which in
youth carried it away from its birthplace, but now that its
mature look like two crimson towels. What shall I do? At
least this carcass allows me to test their resistance to
flame. It is said they clean one another with their flaming
breath. Perhaps this fool thinks hes slain a hatchling king of
lizards, instead of an aged cousin. At least hes saved me a
trip out to the desert. Still, I wish this one was alive. Ive
heard they are of a high animal intelligence and I was sure I
could teach it to play fetch.... -Turgis
Habits/Life Cycle: Fire Drakes breed about once every 10 years
but have clutches of as many as 20-36 eggs at a time.  They are
buried in warm sand or earth and left for 6 months.  At the end
of this time they crack open their shells and dig upward.
Usually, a full quarter do not make it to the surface.  There the
young drake eats whatever it can for a full day.  At the next sun-
rise, the drake will spread its wings and fly 10-60 miles from the
nesting site.  They lose the ability to fly when they reach maturi-
ty at 18-20 years.  Fire Drakes are solitary predators and will
kill other drakes without hesitation.  During the mating season
this solitariness is reversed and they gather in orgiastic mating
groups that can last weeks at a time.  The Fire Drake will mate
with any other Fire Drake during this time and have even been
known to cross bread with other drake types, producing unusual
offspring.  A Fire Drake can live as long as 100 years.
Climate/Habitat: Desert, Highland and Semiarid.  Desert, vol-
canic mountains, wasteland, and exotic (lava). 
Body Type: Giant (reptile with wings) 
STR 17, AGL 15, VIT 17, INT 3, WIS 2, KNW 2, PER 2,
AUR 13, PTY 5, WIL 13 
MR 50%, CR 50%, IR 0%, PR 80%, DR 55% 
END 75, Hide 25 (1 per 3 End), Shock 225 
Melee +0, Missile -2, Damage +15, Defensive +1, Initiative -4 
Height/Length 14 long  Weight 2000 lb. 
Movement
Walk 8/80/8, Trot 16/160/16, Run 24/240/24, Fly 32/320/32 
Alignment Any Neutral
Attacks (Intermediate Attack Skill)  
Bite (3 second, 8d6 cutting)
Claw (3 seconds, 5d6 cutting)
Raking Claw (4 seconds, 6d6 cutting; grappling only)
Tail Slap (4 seconds, 7d6 crushing)
Breath Attack (5 seconds, weak cone of flame, 3d6 (1d6 per 20
End of the fire drake). 
Attack Notes: This drake prefers to corner its victim.  If it thinks
itself superior to its prey, it will chase the frightened victim into
a narrow place before attacking.  Once it has the target cornered,
it utilizes one breath attack and then attacks twice with each
claw, then once with a bite thereafter.  If the drake has only one
opponent, it will attempt to grapple the opponent and then attack
twice with each claw and once with each raking claw.
Experience Value: 6800
Skills: Climb 50%, Force 60%, Berserking 40%.
Abilities: Magical Flight, Breath Attack (fire), Improved Magic
Resistance, Hide (scales), Tail Slap, Medium Fangs, Enhanced
Smell, Heat Sensing, Infravision (60 feet).
Disabilities: Weakness (Cold; x2 damage from any cold
attacks).
Special Notes
Detect Magic:  The fire drake radiates magic to all who sense it.
Tame Animal:  These drakes are tamable at considerable penalty
(-50% to the skill roll).  
Tanning/Armoring:  Once tanned, the supple drake hide makes
excellent armor that looks like scale mail, but is treated as
leather.  This armor retains its immunity to fire for one year
after tanning.  The armor value is equal to the drakes hide value
when it was alive and lasts much longer than normal leather
(even after the fire protection fades).  Its hide radiates a low
level of magic when detected.

Drake, Spitting (Magical Animal, level 7)
These foul drake are a danger to all life.  Even trolls fear
them for they share their home in the murky swamps and
bogs.  The spiting drakes lair is unmistakablethe stench of
decay and the glistening wet bones give away its presence.
The strongest of Goblin Shaman are said to tame them but I
have not seen it.  Thankfully they are the slowest breeders
of any drake and remain the rarest.  Though not the largest
of drakes their acid spit makes them a match for creatures
four times their size.  Goblins speak of seeing small breeds
at about 6 feet long and sighting larger ones at 20 feet in
length.  This drake is seen in two color patternsin marshes
and wetlands they are green with blue markings, but in
swamps they tend to be a loathsome yellow with green high-
lights.  Their worse feature is their acid spit, a glob of which
can cause death in seconds.  I was struck in the arm while
dispatching one of their number.  The acid must be magical
for when I was struck my Aura flashed and I came to no
harm.  One blessing is that this type of drake cannot fly with
his meager wings. -Turgis
Habits/Life Cycle: Spitting drakes breed once every 25 years.
The fertilized female lays 6-8 eggs in warm sandy or earthy pits,
and then covers them, leaving them to grow and hatch on their
own in 6 months time.  When the hatchlings emerge, they
immediately swim off and hunt for food.  If they find none they
will return to the nest and kill the other hatchlings.  
A spitting drake will attack any other creature it sees except an
opposite-sexed spitting drake.  They can live 200 years or more.  
Climate/Habitat: Humid Continental, Humid Coastal,
Subtropical Wet, and Tropical Wet.   Rain forests, swamps and
marshes.  
Body Type: Giant (reptile with atrophied wings)

bite attack thereafter.  If the drake has only one opponent, it will
attempt to grapple the opponent and then attack twice with each

Tame Animal:  These drakes are theoretically tamable with a
penalty of  -80% to the skill roll.  
Potion Lore:  Its ground scales can be used to make a potion of
endure acid.  Four scales are required.  It is the main ingredient.
Tanning/Armoring:  The tanned hide of this drake is used to
make excellent armor that has the protection equal to the amount
that protected the drake and yet is as supple as leather.  This
armor protects its wearer against acid for one year.  The scales
of a drake are more durable than leather and will often outlast
leather.  This hide radiates a low level of magic when detected.

Fox (Animal, level 1)
In many cultures, foxes are seen as tricksters. It is their
fur that is more often sought than their wit, and it may have
earned its legend escaping hunters. Foxes hunt only a few a
square miles, eating fruit and small beasts, and the occasion-

of warring cousins, fox and hound.... -Pock
Habits/Life Cycle: A mated pair of Foxes will breed once a year

learns quickly, hunting alone within six months of birth.  During
winter the family group breaks up and the young seek new terri-
tory for their own.  gestation: 50-60 days 6-13 years.

Frogs and Toads (Animal, level 0)
Personal note: When I was boy,  I would go down to the lake
on warm nights at dusk and close my eyes. Across the
water, I could hear frog song, the little hoppers calling to
prospective mates. A few weeks later, I'd look for tadpoles. If
I could sneak one past mother, I'd even keep one in a bowl and
watch as it grew legs, and later left the bowl. One year, I
watched my tadpole become a toad. I let it go, as I did
always, and watched as it left. It turned from the water,
domain of frogs, and headed towards the brush. A frog, being
a better jumper, might have escaped what came next, namely
a fox darting out, biting, and making my friend a meal. My
first lesson in the food chain. When I see a frog or toad now,
I'm reminded of the lesson... and of how good frog's legs
taste.... -Turgis
Climate/Habitat: Humid Continental, Humid Oceanic, Humid
Subtropical, Tropical Wet and Tropical Wet and Dry.  Coastal,
jungle, marsh, rain forest, river and swamp.

Habits/Life Cycle: Amphibians are born in water and spend
their lives in and around water.  The female lays eggs in water
once per year, where the male then fertilizes them.  Gill
equipped offspring compete in the multitudes for food and
space.  Their primary sources of food are water-based insects
and tiny fish.  When the amphibian reaches maturity its lungs
develop and it is then capable of leaving water to breath air.
Some species breath only on the surface from then on and others
retain the ability to breathe in either environment.

Gargoyle (Magical Animal, level 7)
Far to the west, there's a castle in a mountain pass I used
as shelter from a thunderstorm. Huddled in a room by the
courtyard, I could see shapes on the ramparts, like
grotesque, short, winged men. One moved, I thought. Then one
was upon me, swooping through the window in my sleep,
unleashing a flurry of stone punches and kicks, nearly doing
me in before I could get my sword. I struck and chipped my
blade on stone. It crashed in upon me again. I swung, and this
time caught its neck. That seemed effective. It thrashed and
stomped, turning its fury on the walls themselves. Only then
did I see the rest of them, who fell on the aggressor to the
castle. They bowed afterwards, sensing no malice in me, and I
left them to their endless watch.... - Pock
Habits/Life Cycle: Gargoyles are born when another gargoyle
breaks a large chunk of itself off and lays it in a rockpile or
stone quarry.  The parent gargoyle leaves the piece of itself and
immediately flies off.  Since they can ingest stone through long
contact with stone, a copy is slowly grown.  They can only
reproduce once this way per 20 years.  It takes a full year for the
new gargoyle to grow in this fashion, even after, it has yet to
aspects_rebuild.qxd  11/1/01  7:12 AM  Page 64

preferably one of stone and watch those therein.  If it pleases the
gargoyles quirks it stays and guards, if not it flies off in search
of another place or master.  A being can acquire a gargoyle by
visiting it as it forms over the course of its infant year.  A 
gargoyle can survive a millennium; after that it returns to stone
and quickly breaks into pieces within a year.

Evil and selfish, so arrogant as to ignore the younger races

hoarding of power and wealth. They have lead uprisings when

raids I fought against were their doing. Civilized Giants claim
to have exiled their cloud kin for experiments in dark magic.
They apparently did not cease, despite exile, for they are
the only giants with magic, and they wield great power. They
live in high mountains or disguised cloud enclaves, seeking to
control the elements and increase their magics, which contin-
ues the feud with their civilized cousins, and it is rumored
cause of enmity with Fairies.  Fairies keep their distance
though, alluding to a great catastrophe that might happen if
the two races clash... again. -Pock

adulthood within 200 years of birth.

sat in shock, part of my mind wondering where the magical
stream must be, the one it must drink of to strengthen the
its own bones that, like other giants, support their great

pened. The screeching stopped, a pure voice
sang out, followed by a second and a third. In
no language I had ever heard, in tones that
couldn't be words. But the meaning was clear,
'Come. Come to be loved, come to taste joy.' My
porters dropped my cages and net, running into the
lair. Thankfully, I somehow held back. I heard their flesh
rip as talons, tore them and then all too quiet as the
harpies had finished their work. I am not ashamed to say
I fled. When I did come back with wax-stuffed ears and
got close enough to end them. I can say there is nothing
natural about these monstrosities. They were equal parts
woman and bird--with wings, and bird's talons, a woman's face
but sharp teeth and yellow eyes that revealed only animal
cunning. I shook off the feeling of helplessness and put an end
to them with crossbow and spear. I knew Harpies were too
dangerous even for a freakshow." -Anivar
Habits/Life Cycle: A thoroughly unnatural creature with only
one sex: female. They live for about two dozen years. Once each
season they lay a single egg. The egg is rarely cared for and
most die, occasionally one is laid amongst enough warm carrion
to survive and hatch. The eggs are mottled brown color about a
foot in circumference. Hatchlings survive on rotted meat and
offal until they are six weeks of age. At this stage their wings
have fully formed and they fly far. After a few weeks of travel
they will settle down and become extremely lazy, rarely 
flying away from their nest.
Harpies are not territorial and seem to
prefer the company of other harpies. A
mature harpy prefers the flesh of humanoids
but can survive on any meat. 
Climate/Habitat: Anywhere humanoid prey is
present
Body Type:  Special (bird with humanoid upper
torso, neck and head)

Special Notes 
The Song of Longing: The harpy has one instinctual magical
song that is untranslatable into any humanoid language and can-
not be learned or discovered.  It takes 2-7 seconds to begin
effecting listeners; causing any hearing being under 15 Will
Force to make a Control Resistance or feel compelled to join the
singer. The controlled being believes a fantasy awaits, going
eagerly to meet it.

Hippogryph (Magical Animal, level 5)
I've never known what to think of these, my Baron. It would
be simple to think that once a mage got a hold of a griffon
(See my description in a few pages) and said, "Hey, let's see
if I can make this look even stranger." But, it's more. The
creature is smarter than a griffon, and possesses a more
thinking way of doing things. There's a mind behind those
eyes, even though surrounded in feathers, underlined with a
beak. The foreparts of an eagle (though the plumage is all
wrong) do not match badly with the body of a horse. If you're
good, a hippogryph is a far easier mount to deal with than a
griffon. Perhaps it was some romantic interlude, followed by
a lover's quarrel, that made the griffon hate pegasi and gave
the world these beauties for thinking races to chase after....
-Anivar

Insects
They are ever present in the swamp. They are all around you
on the farm. In trails and cities they hide and thrive wherev-
er you aren't looking. Most times, they are seen as a nui-
sance, a thing to be squashed or swatted. It's not hard to
see what their part is in the delicate balance of life. They
eat crops; they eat the dead; they turn the soil and carry
away bits of refuse. They make honey and feed the animals
we eat. Some impatient people make meals of bugs them-
selves. Sometime, sit by a tree and watch these little lives
go on. Pay attention to things on six-legs; learn from their
industriousness. Take care not to upset the balance, and
respect that they will thrive, even when we die and are food
for them. -Harkis
Insect (Animal, level 0)
Habits/Life Cycle: Insects are found in every nook and cranny
in every environment.  They do congregate in greater numbers
in wet environments.  Most have a two-stage life cycle spending
early life in larval form and then morphing into an adult form at
maturity.  Many insects lead singular existences and others form
great collectives or colonies.  The greatest ant colonies can have
over a million members.  Few insects live longer than a year and
most mere months. 
Climate/Habitat: All but the most exotic (lava, space/ether, etc.)
Body Type: Tiny (insect)

Insect Varieties
The following are presented as templates for the nearly limitless
variety of insects:
Web Weaving Insects
This variety of insects weave silky strands for a variety of rea-
sons.  Some make cocoons, others homes, and some even make
elaborate traps, as the spider does. None are able to weave as
efficiently or with different webs as the spider.  
Additional Evoke Package: Web Weaving (Strong)
Water Insects 
This type of insect makes its home on top of or in the water, sur-
viving by feeding on other insects, plant life or even tiny fish.
Evoke Package: Hide (Chiteneous), Instinct (food/water),
Jumping, Enhanced Disease Resistance, Climbing, Speed, High
Pain Threshold, Insect Limbs, Maw (Mandibles), Agility, Diving,
Swimming.
Venomous Insects 
There are few insects that carry a toxin and even fewer that have
one that is a danger to humanoids.  Those that do either inject it
with their bite or they use a poison stinger.  This variety of
insect is considered to be level 1 because of the mild threat it
poses to humanoids.
Evoke Package: Hide (Chiteneous), Instinct (food/water),
Jumping, Enhanced Disease Resistance, Climbing, Speed, High
Pain Threshold, Insect Limbs, Maw (Mandibles), Agility, Venom,
Weak; poison I-V, potency D) or (Venom, Strong; poison VI-X,
potency L).

Disease Bearing Insects 
This insect is a carrier for disease.  Some carry diseases that
affect only one type of humanoid, others carry diseases that
affect a wide variety of humanoids and animals.  Usually insects
that drain the blood of humanoids and animals are the type that
can leave behind a disease.  Because of the threat of their dis-
ease, these insects are considered level 1 rather than level 0.
Evoke Package: Hide (Chiteneous), Instinct (food/water),
Jumping, Enhanced Disease Resistance, Climbing, Speed, High
Pain Threshold, Insect Limbs, Maw (Mandibles), Agility,
Disease Bearing (carries random disease).
Ant (Animal, level 0)
My master saw me go to swat my wrist. He caught my
hand, and asked what I thought to crush. A mere red ant, I
said. He said Yes, just an ant. And with a hive, a child-killer.
And in a carpet an acre wide, a danger to forest and village.
He scooped it off with a knife and set it free in solemn
respect.... -Harkis

never saw me. Sorry that I couldn't say goodbye to his

Manticore (Magical Animal, level 8)
I can write few words about such creatures without bile, but
I will tell you that which might save your life. You may recog-
nize it by the features of other beasts: face of a human,
wings of a bat, body of a lion and the tail of a dragon, tipped
with spikes that it may hurl at enemies it perceives as great
threats. It has two rows of teeth,; with these tiny daggers,
as well as its claws and spikes, it is able to kill its weight in
foes when enraged. Even when hunting, it kills anything it finds
and eats only warm flesh, gorging itself at any opportunity.
Its greatest weakness is that it is still only a beast, with a
beasts instinct not intellect. If its wings (spanning 24 to 48
feet) shadow your path, grab the largest weapon you can find
and hope you can take shelter. -Pock
Habits/Life Cycle: The manticore goes where it will, eats any-
thing that moves for a few weeks and then moves on.  Between
feedings it likes to find a cave or out of the way shelter and
sleep for several days.  This is the only time the creature is
vulnerable.  Manticores mate once per year, when they can find
mates at all.  Their mating is difficult at bestone of the part-
ners is sometimes killed and even when both male and female

survive the flying, ripping ordeal, fertilization is only a 1 in 10

Mantis, Giant (Magical Animal, level 4)
The first mantis I saw was about palm length, and sat
methodically nibbling at the head of a wasp, killing it by inches.

when I saw one pony-sized, I still could only watch. But the
meal was a man. The only difference was the wasp didn't
scream.... -Harkis
Habits/Life Cycle: During mating, the female giant mantis often

ment of the egg case, thus it is often raised by other creatures.
The giant mantis is a strict carnivore.

Nightmare (Magical Animal, level 6)
In the light of dusk, we thought it a torch bearer upon a
black mare, but that illusion faded.  I saw hardened troops
scatter before hooves that burnt grass, before eyes that
tore at the heart. I barely stood my ground as it leapt
impossibly over pike and sword to smash our forward cata-
pult and the crew that manned it. It danced in triumph, and I
could see it was not animalistic thrashing, but a message:
You cannot stop me.  Next it charged our weapons tent, run-
ning down a few unfortunate souls before smashing a barrel
of oil. The oil blazed, lighting the tent and shrouding the foul
horse-thing in a cloak of fire. That image, that black equine
form surrounded by flame, head raised in mockery, never
leaves my dreams, never lets me forget the name
'Nightmare'.... -Harkis

Peryton (Magical Animal, level 4)
I thought, when I saw the shadow across the path, that I
might meet a companion for my journeys. It had been a long
time since I'd spoken to a Thinking Race. When I turned the
corner, my blood ran cold. It sat on a stone, its shadow noth-
ing like its body: The head and legs of a magnificent stag, its
body, wings and green feathers of a bird of prey. It regarded
me for a moment, and I fingered my sword. I called on the
power to speak to beasts, and asked it "Friend or Foe."  It
regarded me coldly and said, "You are not human.  You may
go." I paused. "You cannot hurt me," it said, and emphasized
this by dashing an antler on a rock, which began to grow back
as I watched. I left, and only then did I think on the pile of
skulls beneath its perch. -Pock
Climate/Habitat: Mountains, forests, hills.
Body Type: Medium (hybrid: quadruped plus the tail and wings
of bird)

I used to never think about walking on grass. Plants were
just scenery, something to be walked upon or eaten in salad.
My master instilled in me the respect for their green lives
though.  He told me things that I would see in action later, in
my journeys. It went beyond knowing what you could eat or
not eat but also what might eat me. Not every danger to
man moves around on legs. Some things just sit in a garden,
bask in the sun and grow, heedless to the fleshy bits around
them, until one day, when something starts to get all superi-
or because it's ambulatory, and sits down on the wrong patch
of green. Then, a life more simple might leap up, wrap a vine
around you, and make you what all flesh becomes when buried: 
fertilizer.... -Harkis
Brown Creeper (Plant, level 1)
I thought at first it was a snake. When Toftin lifted it off
his neck, I thought it was but a desiccated vine. Toftin
tossed it,  began to swoon and Sergeant Wiskin caught him
before he fell.  Sergeant had us pull back, while pulling a small
green leaf from the vine and putting it in a vial. I thought I
saw the vine try to pull away. As he navigated us around the
spot, Sergeant explained that what it was, not a dead plant,
but a living predatory vine. "It senses heat like a snake," he
said, "falls on a creature, poisons it to sleep, like Toftin. Then
it wraps around the prey, drains it of blood. Creeps back up
into the trees." He held up his vial. "This 'll be safe unless it
drinks. Then it would grow. At three feet, it'll kill a fox. At fif-
teen... well." As if planned, we passed a cow's bones, laced in
brown.... -Harkis

Mold, Giant (Plant, level 3)
"Basic swamp survival," my master said, "know where to
sleep." He was giving this lecture standing on the trunk of a
fallen cypress, looking down on some bone and bits of armor.
They were arranged as if the person once around and inside
them had been simply dissolved away. "It may look like an
inviting mattress, but that burnt umber mat down there is a
living colony. Cut it, poke it nothing happens. Smash it, it'll feel
it. Sleep on it, it'll wake up, even after being dormant for
months, to poison and liquefy you, turning flesh into food to
begin a new colony. Most often, it's the already wounded who
are in danger. It doesn't move much. But find some of it on
you, and there's a sure way to remove it." He tossed a torch
down into it then, and it lit up like a pool of oil.... -Harkis

Mushroom, Giant (Plant, level 2)
I like mushrooms. We always had them on the farm. There
was a neighbor who grew them in a cave and traded us for
meat. They need no light to grow, only good soil. What's the
phrase? "They treat me like a mushroom, keep me in the dark
and feed me manure." He used to tell us stories about how
down in the realms of under dwellers or in dense forest,
some less scrupulous folk cultivated forests of huge ones,
some ten feet high. Those mushrooms, he said, had a tough
skin like bark, but made for good eating for dozens. The draw-
back? They cough poison spores, when touched or at random.
But this is good for the mushroom: The best fertilizer for
them is rotting flesh, tilled into the soil. Remember, few visi-
tors come back from under realms.... -Harkis
Habits/Life Cycle:  It grows from seed spores released near the
end of a three to four year life span.  The giant mushroom grows
two to three feet per year until the end of its life span.

Plants, Magical
A Ranger's place is in nature, amongst the forest, fighting
against wild things for the sake of the innocent or fighting
against the ignorant for the sake of wild things. The more
rare duty is to fight against monsters. You can see, in your
mind's eye, one of my brothers at the base of a great tree,
crouched in green armor, bow in hand, ready to defend the
forest from all invaders and fools. But life has taught me
much. The forest itself, not just its inhabitants, are to be
respected. Not simply because they are living and deserve
the respect due living things, but because things that seem
to be no more than plants can quickly turn out to be an ally-
or a foe. -Harkis
Blood Vine (Magical Plant, level 2)
I remember being warned about great constrictor snakes
and how at first glance one might think them a vine entwined
about a limb. I wish my master would have thought to warn
me of the dangers of the vines themselves. There is a scar
on my arm that I would have been spared had he granted me
this wisdom. Perhaps I would have avoided the perils of the
vine's acid as well, the magical blood sap spilling forth as I
hastily brought down my sword. I never expected to have to
defend against a razor-leafed whip that could wield itself and
never imagined a plant would need a weekly meal of flesh to
survive.  I would rather face a snake than the mouth of that
purple red plant again.... -Harkis
Habits/Life Cycle:  A blood vine must digest animal tissues no
less than once per week to sustain its nervous system but supple-
ments this with a weakened form of photosynthesis.  Once every
seven years the strongest vine from the plant grows longer than
the rest and begins growing its own juvenile brain.  The new
blood vine will quickly detach itself from the parent and move
away.
Climate/Habitat:  Forests, plains, marshes, swamps, hills and
brushlands (Coastal, Humid Continental and Humid Oceanic
climes)

Drowning Kelp (Magical Plant, level 2)
Plants that seek to consume flesh are not limited to land. In
marshes, lakes and slow moving rivers, one can find a green
yellow plant, like sea-kelp but very hard to see in murky
water, that will wait for something warm to swim above it. It
wraps its fronds around its victim, squeezes until the prey
stops moving, and drags the victim to the bottom to be
consumed, gnawed sometimes of the course of a week by six
small sucker-like mouths. It is not malicious, but the small
mind at the bottom of the plant is mostly concerned with
eating, and will strike a fish or cow as quickly as an elf. This
is a saving grace because the plant will only strike one crea-
ture at a time. I only needed to see one demonstration, how-
ever, to start thinking twice about swimming in murky water.

Enveloper Grass (Magical Plant, level 3)
The struggle for food is never a question of cruelty, but
some creatures simply do not fight fair. Once I sat near a
clearing against a tree and looked out over the sun-kissed
grass. Near the center was a patch of darker grass. My
attention, however, was on a deer, a doe that began to graze.
I stilled myself as to not disturb such timid beauty. It
stepped forward and sniffed at the darker patch of grass
and walked forward until it got to the center. There, she con-
tinued no further, for the patch of grass snapped up like a
ball, a spongy smothering blanket. The ball rolled around for a
few moments, due to the thrashing inside. Soon the move-
ment stopped, and in minutes, the ball opened. Nothing was
left of the deer, and the patch seemed a little larger.
-Harkis
Habits/Life Cycle:  Survives by photosynthesis, but can only
grow by absorption of animal tissues.  When the plant grows too
large it splits into equal-sized halves.  One of the two rolls into a
ball and randomly rolls away for several hours, finally stopping
and unrolling far from the first Enveloper.

Heartwood Tree (Magical Plant, level 5)
He was named Schonal, an unsavory fellow. Alongside the
wagon,  I saw him eating a golden apple. It seemed the wrong
season, and he said a woman was selling them on the side of
road at the last stop. Three hours later he doubled over in
pain. I came to his side, but he stood and looked euphoric.
Soon after he went off to relieve himself, but never came
back. I left the caravan to track him and followed to where
he bought the apple. Schonal was sitting before a huge tree,
heavy with golden fruit. The woman was there, a dog and a
few others. I cursed as my teachers words came to me too
late. Each was now slave to the tree, its seeds burrowed
into their spines, and would fight to the death for it.  Theyd
offer its fruit to strangers. I vowed to gather men and
return, to kill the brain-fruit on the top of the tree... if I could
get past Schonal. -Harkis
Habits/Life Cycle:  This tree blooms in spring but always has
one large fruit containing its brain.  The brain fruit is generally
the same as the other fruit except that its attachment to the tree
is much more secure and is always found on the highest
branches.

Close inspection reveals it is shaped less regularly than the its

Silver Tree (Magical Plant, level 10)
My father was high born, a Duke. Only once were we called to
the High Kings court. I mean not the regional king, but the
lord of all Wood Elves. We were prepared by our house mage
and left via magic. When I opened my eyes, I saw the most
beautiful thing in the world. In our language, they are called
Elasarin, the Ancient Ones. The smallest is said to be two
hundred feet high. Here there was a circle of them, the only
way they will grow, and one stood over 800 feet, the one in
whose 50-foot-wide branches the High Kings castle was
perched. In dawns light, the Elasarins bark scintillated like sil-
ver and I could only stand in awe. My father whispered that
these trees were immortal, and the space within their circle
sacred, a thing to be defended no matter the cost.... -Harkis
Habits/Life Cycle:  The circulation of the tree is slow, as long as
a nearby water source exists for any of the Silver Trees in a
grove, all can share its water through their entwined root system.
A Silver Tree seed is a thing of legend.  Druids of the highest
order, tending the trees, are unable to draw the fruit forth from
the tree.  If the tree is awakened, and it decides to, it will pro-
duce a single giant acorn in its highest branch.  It can only pro-
duce the seed once per eon.  Getting the seed to grow requires
that at least eleven other seeds planted in a circle in the newest
spring.  The ground must be blessed or magical, near a source of
blessed or magical water.  If the circle is irregular, the source of
water not strong enough or the ground not rich enough, the trees
will not grow.  If these conditions are met, one hundred years
from the date of the planting the saplings will burst from the
ground.  Each year they will grow visibly, until reaching their
maturity at two hundred years.
Climate/Habitat:  Exotic (magical Forest) in Humid Oceanic
and Humid Continental
Body Type:  Special (roots x1, low trunk x3, medium trunk x2,
head area x1, high trunk x1, branches (only limb))

that there are tree folk who respect only Grents and seek

Grents know how new Tree Stalkers and Tree Walkers are

Roc (Magical Animal, level 6)
Yes, I can say I've seen one. A herder thought someone was
stealing his cattle. I was sent to stand vigil, hidden in the
woods. I watched the cows as they slept or grazed, and won-
dered how no tracks had been found. How was the thief get-
ting in without a trace? My answer came as the faintest
whisper on the wind, turned to a roar, as a bird so large as to
blot out the stars swooped down and scooped up a steer as
a hawk might snag a rabbit. The bird soared away effortless-
ly, high into the night sky. I felt my bow go limp in my hands,
as the roc dropped the bull, letting it fall mooing to the
ground. Another graceful swoop and it carried the dead cow
away. In the morning I told the rancher either gather many
ballistae or move the herd elsewhere. -Harkis

berserk effect has worn off, the being must make a Control
Resistance each 10 seconds (for the next 24 hours) or fall into a
deep sleep.  The victim will then die in 1d4 hours, and the body
will dissolve into a fertilizer.  All slime bag pieces still attached
to the being will feed on this fertilzer and grow into full adult
slimes. 
Experience Value: 900
Abilities: Vibration Sense, Hide (Fibrous), Nervous System, Life
Sense, Mobility, Mucous (on stalks), Irritant (berserk), parasitic
conversion (food)
Disabilities: Blind
Immunities: Shock, Disease.
Special Notes
Regeneration: When reduced to negative vitality, the creature
releases its spores and splits into two 40 Endurance bags.  A
Strength save must be made to shake each of these off, every 10
seconds.  These when split will then become two 20 Endurance
slimes, and when split these turn into two 10 Endurance ver-
sions.  The process ends here; any 10 Endurance slimes will be
killed when reduced to negative Vitality.  
Swamp Beast (Slime, level 6)
I think the thing trying to kill us in the swamp uses one of
these as its guardian. What deal they have worked out is
beyond me, but I'm sure of it. It stands like a man, but it is
made up slimy plant fiber. Toftin found it first, when it
spewed foul spores, making him gag at the water's edge. We
barely pulled him from the thing's claws and fangs. We struck
it in the place it should have a head, but it just grew back. It
has one vital organ, maybe its brain. I know cause I struck it,
but when I struck the same place again, I realized it could
move that one vital area. We chased it off with torches. Fire
seems to be something this half-beast, half-plant nightmare
doesn't like. I hope I can make use of that fact when I face it
again.... -Harkis

Snake, Giant (Magical Animal, level 5)
I nearly died getting ours. Coils, circling me from head to
toe, squeezed my breath from me. One eye the size of a
buckler watched with inhuman patience for me to die as the
mage cast a cold spell and I passed out. I live, but in any dark
tree or deep rocky crevice I expect to see another pair of
those eyes, a glint of inhuman scale, just waiting to slither
and strike.... -Anivar
Habits/Life Cycle: The Giant Snake spends most of its life
sleeping and needs to eat only about once a week or so.  Every
year they molt, shedding their skin against rock or tree.  While
they are molting and for the week thereafter their skin is soft,
losing half of its protectiveness.  They can mate once a year,
laying clutches of 6-16 eggs.  Early mornings and late after-
noons they seek the sun, even the ocean dwellers.  Sea snakes
breath through nostrils just as their land based kin do but can
stay submerged for up to eight hours at a time through the spe-
cial membranes of their mouth that allow some respiration.  At
night or in cold conditions they are torpid and move at half
speed.
Climate/Habitat: Any save Highland or Icecap, any save exotic
and ocean (below surface).

tabby, with a full set of wings on its shoulders. It followed its

Spiders
The title perfect predator too often gets thrown around in
zoological circles. After all many creatures are built for it,
and two legged hunters clutch their crafted tools and merely
stand in awe. But if they were to turn to their own attics
and gardens, then hunter and sage might see the true holder
of the title. The champion has 8 legs and 8 eyes. It might
weave webs, or simply be fast. It might have poison. They can
see behind them, sense slight vibration with the whiskers
covering their bodies. A spider can tell the difference
between a fly and bit of garbage stuck in its web without
ever moving from the center. No matter their size, humans
and other speaking races have offered them respect and
fear for as long as they have known of them. It is shame I
must dissect mine.... -Turgis
Habits/Life Cycle: The spider thrives in warm wet areas where
insects dwell in abundance.  Web weaving spiders can travel
when young by creating a balloon of web silk and catching the
wind.  The varieties that migrate by this means usually do so
once.  Most spiders live a little over a year, females laying an
egg sac of thousands of eggs as they near death.  
Climate/Habitat: All climates except icecap and tundra. All
habitats except exotic and deep wet ones such ocean/sea floor.

Unicorn (Magical Animal, level 8)
As a boy, a mere 35 years old,  I took a wineskin, some
bread and a sausage and crept out a window one night,  to
the grove no one would explain well.  My shelter was a bush,
and there I stayed, eating and waiting. Untold time passed; I
was tired, but then I heard the softest strike of hoof upon
grass and through the leaves I saw him. He was pure, visibly,
inexplicably. I then knew he was aware of me. Those eyes
watched me, framed in spotless white, crowned with a single
horn. Tufts of fur adorned his chin and above his hooves. I did
not wish to ride him, feeling unworthy to even think of touch-
ing such majesty. It took all my strength, but I bowed my
head, and he did the same before galloping off. The next thing
I knew was my father's hands, which lifted me from where I
had lay for, as they said, six days.... -Anvar
Habits/Life Cycle:  The Unicorn is ageless once matured, essen-
tially immortal, but their health is linked to the forests they
inhabit.  When it dies, the forest dies as well.  There are a pre-
cious few in any setting and, like the maids they adore, they are
mostly chaste.  A Unicorn birth is possible but rare.  When
Unicorns mate successfully, the male usually withers and dies
within days, only surviving if the female is in some danger or if
there is a perceived threat to distract its death instinct.  The
female carries the foal for almost a full year before birth.  Twin
foals are rare and thus populations decrease over time.  
Climate/Habitat:  Exotic (magical forest) in Humid Oceanic and
Humid Continental
Body Type:  Huge (quadruped)
STR 20, AGL 20, VIT 20, INT 18, WIS 19, KNW 15,
PER N/A, MYS 18
MR 75%, CR Immune, IR Immune, PR 80%, DR Immune
END 120, Hide 24 (1 per 5 End), Shock Immune 
Melee +9, Missile N/A, Damage +31,
Defensive -8, Initiative -9

Weasel (Animal level 0)
Some beasts just get a bad reputation, because they dont
stand and face up to bears, and they are considered cow-
ards. Prey on a weak creature, and get labelled a pest.
Weasels may be bane of the chicken coop, but their speed
and tenacity keep down the vermin population, ridding the land
of excess insects, mice, lizards, etc. They are superb tunnel
fighters and will use their agility to wrestle with creatures,
killing prey with small nips of sharp teeth. They stand on
their hind legs often, (query: Aspirations to sentience?) but
mostly are only seen as a dart of fur or a head above a bush.
Its not enough they are reviled for their hunting practices;
but they are also hunted for fur. Its a shame their glorified
cousins get all the good mentions in bards songs. -Turgis

much magical or blessed water.  A typical horn produces 18-36
ounces of ground powder.  Two ounces is considered to be a
normal dosage for hermetic or potion use.  Only a chaste and
virginal maid may even attempt to tame a Unicorn and then at 
-20%.  Once tame, only she can ride it but she may not utilize
tack of any kind.

Will O Wisp (Magical, level 8)
My son, there is a song that the elves sing. Learn from it:
Party light, living lantern, bobbing up and down
Blue and yellow, dancing, dancing in lightning
Storms are sure whenever we see you come around
Though you never mean to be frightening
Crackle crack, hiss hiss pop, such a peculiar sound
But no words, to yourself you are a-keeping
Don't draw a sword, or they find you fallen ground
It was scared, but you are ever sleeping. -Pock
Climate/Habitat: Dormant will o wisps hide in metal rock and
metallic objects waiting for the next chance to find electricity.
The dormant form is pure static electricity with a detectable
magical essence which allows for thought.

Wolf (Animal, level 2)
Those who are chosen by the Wolf, having him as totem, are
often called to frenzy by howls in the night. Once, camped
alone, I relented and answered, running off to become part of
the pack. We ran through the dark forest, moving as one. I
was accepted as a beta, one of the pack who is to take the
place of the alpha, should he ever seem weak. There was no
need for such a challenge that night, and I was glad. A beta
who fails is turned out from the pack. That night, there was
only the hunt. Our prey was a group of deer, and I fell in with
the group that came from the side, distracting the target
while others came in from the front and struck. I felt anger
against those who blame wolves for missing livestock, know-
ing my brothers would have to be far more desperate to
seek domestic prey.... -Pock
Habits/Life Cycle: Wolves come into season and breed once per
year.  The mother, and often the whole pack, raise the Wolf
pups.  The pack centers around a primary breeding pair between
the alpha male and alpha female.  Not every wolf in a pack will
be mated but those that do, do so for life.  If a mate dies it is
likely the wolf will never mate again. 
Climate/Habitat: Forest, scrublands, mountains or plains.
Body Type: Medium (quadruped)

Wolf, Dire (Magical Animal, level 4)
It may seem strange, as my totem is wolf, but I hate these
creatures. Mounts to goblins, they have spread fear of wolf
kind wherever they have been found. Many cold nights have I
faced them, stared into eyes that seem to glow red in the
darkness. Were they mere beasts, I could not hate them so,
but they are so much more. They border on intelligence, lack-
ing any wisdom or compassion to guide their cunning, but still
understand goblin's doggerel.  They are an affront to nature,
descended of creatures twisted by magery in the time
before humanoids could write.  Natural creatures know this
and run at the scent of them. It is little defense, though, as
Dire Wolf, or Warg in goblin-speak, are able to pick up scents
on the wind from a mile away. Never has a creature been so
worthy of hunting.... -Pock

Habits/Life Cycle: Dire Wolves breed once per year in the
spring time.  The mother is usually driven off by the wolfpack
as soon as the cubs are born, some even eat the offspring.  The
pack, if it can be called that, is lead by whoever is strongest at
that moment.  This leader is constantly challenged and rarely
lasts more than a season as the packs head.  Thus Dire Wolf
packs break up, splinter into several packs and rejoin with other
strays in a never ending cycle that is both vicious and wasteful
of resources.  They will also over hunt any area they are in,
killing even when not hungry.  A Dire Wolf can live as long as
60 years.

Worms
Few people think on worms. They are after all, to the city
dweller, disgusting. To the farmer, though, they are helpers,
making the soil rich for crops. To a fisherman, they are like a
sacrifice, placed upon a hook to summon a fish. Many miracles
come from a creature that is mostly a mobile digestive
track. For them, to move is to eat, ingesting some of the
soil they part with their rhythmic motions. But not all worms
are small or helpful. Some are parasites. They can be scary,
as it's hard to fight a creature that has made its way under
your flesh. I'd rather fight a giant than a parasitic infection.
-Pock
Habits/Life Cycle: In early embryonic development of most
common worms, the mouth and the anus are undifferentiated.
Some are so simple they dont even have a digestive track and
absorb nutrients directly from their surroundings.  Many worms

variety.

Worm, Giant (Magical Animal, level 6)
Underdwellers used these 12 to 48 foot monsters to make
their homes, tamed to carve tunnels with their huge maw.
Faced with one, stay still. Only able to sense vibration,  it
might miss you. Move, and you must get at least 3 of 5
hearts to stop beating or be swallowed, and your remains
cast into the earth it seals in its path. -Pock
Habits/Life Cycle: The giant worm eats rock, earth, flesh, or
whatever passes its maw, its adhesive mucus seals and finishes
creation of tunnel complexes, that eventually become miles
long.  Occasional piles of rich earth can be seen in their tunnels.
These are called casings, and while they represent the worms
excrement they provide a rich soil for growing of crops, magical
or otherwise.  The mucus will eventually become brittle and
cause these tunnels to collapse 50 to 300 years after the worms
passing, depending on the hardness of the soil.  It is a true
aspects_rebuild.qxd  11/1/01  7:13 AM  Page 127

===== PAGE 136 =====
128
hermaphrodite, each being both male and female and can breed
with any other giant worm.  These creatures mate with their
heads pointed away.  Both worms secrete mucus, covering each
other with a slime tube for the last third of their segments,
then fertilization occurs and they separate.  Later, one or both of
the worms secrete a second mucus layer that connects to the first
and slides off the front as the creature backs up, gathering the
fertilized eggs from the previous slime layer into a capsule, that
slowly hardens.  One or two worms will hatch from the cocoon
in 5-6 weeks.  These creatures can only mate once per year and
only do so if they chance upon each other.  Each worm can live
45 to 50 years.

Habits/Life Cycle: Wyverns mate and lay eggs once per 5 years.

spots after each successful mating season.  The wyvern protects

full years to grow to maturity.  During the first year they feed
near the nest.  The second year the fend for themselves and our
encouraged to fly at least 5-10 miles or more away from the nest
site.  They are seen as a serious menace by most humanoids and
are often hunted.  Well organized Goblin clans use them to scout
and for leaders mounts.  Wyverns live approximately 85 years.

"We chased him down late one night for he'd dared to been seen in our streets. My brother threw the first and, as it turned out, only

pry bar fell from my fingers as my feet carried me to the water's edge.  Even after diving, I felt the claws ripping at me. I bear the
scar to this day. My brother, what remained, we buried. Now I fear the streetfear I will hear the screech again and the flapping of
fleshy wings."

will never rest at ease in this life again."

Eliana Yestellian (In humanoid form, 3rd Title)  
"As children we hunted the forest together. She went to war against the desert people. When she came back she was changed 
moody, quiet and introspective. I thought it was the fighting. Foolishly I tried to get her to hunt with me. She agreed and we stalked
the paths again, in search of wolves or other predators. She sniffed at the air, something I had never seen her do before. Turning, she
led us down a side trail towards the highlands. Then, a change took her. Skin hardening, fur growing, eyes widening, nose flattening,
she became something else. A cougar sprang onto the path ahead of us. It seemed agitated but wasn't moving closer. She snarled,
and hissed at it. It turned tail and ran. She bounded after it and that's the last I saw of her. She must think she is sparing us, but the
green fathers could remove the beast's curse."
Race: Wood Elf       Class: Ranger       
Exp: 38,000     Title: 3rd/Tracker

Derek Lithendar (In humanoid form, 3rd Title)  
"Trailing a torn dress, she ran to the temple for sanctuary. A young layman met her at the door. He frowned at her condition, and
offered a warm hand up the old stair. To her horror, the hand grew furry. Nails became talons, his eyes narrowed and yellowed.  His
body spasmed and rippled under the white robes. The hand that offered safety became the claw that tore at her. Her original attackers
saved her life. They hadn't stopped chasing her even to the church. The wolfish monster turned and devoured them, she ran, and ran
and never looked back."

New Spells
Aura Level 3
MASK ODOR
Type:  (War)
Distance to Focus:  Touch
Magic Name:  Grenda Frint
Save:  N/A
Memorization: 15 Minutes
Range:  None
Casting Time: 10 Seconds
Failure:  2% per Aura
Area Affected: 1 Being/Object
Duration: 30 Mins. per Aura 
Description: This spell masks all odors given off by a being and her
possessions, or if cast on a single object, that objects smell is masked.
Alternately, it could be used to change the odor given off by a particu-
lar being to something completely different. 
Notes: No trace of the objects true odor is left behind. It is completely
masked by this spell. 
Aura Level 4
MASK HEAT
Type: (War)
Distance to Focus:  Touch
Magic Name: Grenda Nama
Save:  N/A
Memorization: 35 Minutes
Range:  None
Casting Time: 1 Minute
Failure:  2% per Aura
Area Affected: 1 Being/Object
Duration: 30 Mins. per Aura 
Description: When cast, this causes one being or object to appear visu-
ally equal in apparent temperature to the air around it.  The being or
object is thus undetectable to infravision. Alternately, a different heat
could be set to be given off, ice cold or very hot, etc.
Notes: The objects true temperature is not altered in anyway. When
this spell is cast on very hot objects, whose heat would be visible even
to the naked eye, this heat is masked to sight only. The object or being
will still feel very hot.

Aura Level 7
CALL FAMILIAR  
Type: Sum, Alt, Tra
Distance to Focus: 1 Mile/Aura
Magic Name: Rogul Ravna-Rojor Save: N/A
Memorization: 35 minutes        Failure: 5% per Aura
Casting Time: 1 Hour
Range: Self
Area Affected: 1 Being        Duration: 1 Day per Aura
Description: By means of this spell the caster calls an animal or magical
animal and transforms it temporarily into a familiar. An animal drawn
randomly from within one mile of caster per Aura point and comes
immediately to the caster. At 10 or higher a specific animal type can be
called (if it exists in the area of effect.) At 15 or higher Aura an individ-
ual, known, animal can be called as a familiar. The animal can weigh no
more than 5 pounds per Aura used in the spell. While the spell is in
effect, the familiar desires to be in the presence of the caster and has a
strong affinity for the caster at all times. Besides the animals natural
abilities, additional abilities are gained when the animal becomes a
familiar.
Additional Familiar Powers/Abilities (temporary):
Enhanced Endurance (the creatures Endurance is doubled)
Enhanced Resistances (Magic and Control Resistance are now equal to
the casters)
Endow Intelligence (the familiar has the same mental attributes as the
caster)
Magic Voice
Message 3 x day
Telepathy (with caster) 
Summon (animals type) once per day
Caster Powers/Abilities (temporary):
One point of Endurance is gained per 10 pounds of the familiar.
One sense is enhanced per 5 Aura used in the spell as if under the influ-
ence of the spell Enhance Sight, Smell, etc. (the familiar must possess
the sense.)
One natural power, ability or immunity of the familiar is also granted to
the caster per 4 Aura used in the spell.
Notes: A caster can never have more than one familiar at a time. If the
familiar is killed, the caster loses one point of Will Force permanently.
The caster must make a Vitality save or lose 1d4 Endurance permanently.
The caster also makes a Vitality save to avoid a heart attack as defined in
the Players Guide.

RIPEN SCENT

Save: Magic Resistance

Uses: 3 per Piety Control 
Invoking Time: 5 seconds
Range: None
Area Affected: One being/object Duration: 1 hour/PC
Distance to Focus: 3 per PC
Description: One specific scent is amplified, providing a +25% bonus
to the Smell skill to follow or identify only that exact scent.
Piety Level 2
SHROUD SCENT
Alignment Restriction: Neutral Save: Magic Resistance
Prayer Time: 10 minutes
Uses: 3 per Piety Control 
Invoking Time: 10 seconds
Range: None
Area Affected: 1 being/object
Duration: 15 min/PC
Distance to Focus: 2 per PC
Description: When cast on a being, this invocation masks all odors
emitted by a being and her possessions.  When cast on an object, no
odors are emitted by that object.
Notes: Practitioners of the Smell skill have a 0% skill chance to detect
the target of this invocation.  The target of the invocation retains its
own sense of smell.
Piety Level 3
BREATH OF THE TURTLE
Alignment Restriction: Neutral Save: Magic Resistance
Prayer Time: 15 minutes
Uses: 2 per Piety Control 
Invoking Time: 10 seconds
Range: None
Area Affected: Self
Duration: 15 min/PC
Distance to Focus: 2 per PC
Description: Target being may hold his breath three times as long as
normal without suffering asphyxiation.
Notes: See the Masters Manual for rules on asphyxiation.
EYES OF THE EAGLE
Alignment Restriction: Neutral Save: Magic Resistance

Uses: 1 per Piety Control 
Invoking Time: 10 seconds
Range: None
Area Affected: One object
Duration: Instantaneous
Distance to Focus: 2 per Piety Control
Description: Restores one point of object strength per Piety Control to
the targeted non-magical leather object.
Notes: Unless the invoker successfully makes an appropriate social
skill roll for the item being repaired, it will be quite obvious that the
item was repaired by an unskilled person.  Any repaired area will be
barren of any creative detail. However, an appropriate successful social
skill roll indicates the item repaired will appear as if it had never been
damaged.

Piety Level 9
ASPECT OF THE BEAST
Alignment Restriction: Neutral Save: Magic Resistance
Prayer Time: 45 minutes
Uses: 1 per 4 Piety Control 
Invoking Time: 10 seconds 
Range: None
Area Affected: 1 object
Duration: 10 min/PC
Distance to Focus: 2 per Piety Control
Description: A sculpture or carving of a creature becomes animated.  If
the object is a complete likeness of an animal, the animal will have full
movement capabilities.  The creature is not alive and does not have any
powers or abilities.
Notes: Using this invocation, a staff with a snakes head could be trans-
formed into an animated snake with full movement.  Likewise, a carv-
ing of a lions head on a shield would allow the lions head to snap at
combatants in which the wielder is engaged. In order to repair damage,
magic items need Mend or Repair cast upon them, non-magic items
may use Mold and appropriate social skill for the material type
(Metalworking, etc.).

Alignment Restriction: Neutral Save: Control Resistance
Prayer Time: 15 minutes
Uses: 2 per Piety Control 
Invoking Time: 5 seconds
Range: None
Area Affected: 1 Slime/2 PC
Duration: 1 min/PC
Distance to Focus: 5 per Piety Control
Description: Causes one Slime to obey the mental commands of the
invoker.  
Notes: Lesser slimes are automatically controlled while greater slimes
get a Control Resistance.  Arch slimes cannot be controlled.

HAILSTORM
Alignment Restriction: Neutral Save: None
Prayer Time: 60 minutes 
Uses: 1 per 5 Piety Control 
Invoking Time: 10 seconds
Range: None
Area Affected: 100 ft. radius/PC Duration: Instantaneous
Distance to Focus: 3 per Piety Control
Description: As long as partial cloud cover exists, the invoker can
form a thunderhead to devastate an area with hail.  All beings in the
area affected are pelted with 2d4 hailstones.  Each hailstone inflicts 2d4
damage per 5 Piety Control.
Notes: Hailstorm cannot be invoked indoors or underground.
Piety Level 13
BOIL EARTH
Alignment Restriction: Neutral Save: None
Prayer Time: 65 minutes 
Uses: 1 per 6 Piety Control 
Invoking Time: 10 seconds 
Range: None
Area Affected: 5 ft. radius/PC
Duration: 20 seconds
Distance to Focus: 5 per Piety Control
Description: The area affected becomes superheated and molten.  Any
beings within the area affected begin sinking into the ground.  Any
exposed body area coming in contact with the boiling earth is inflicted
with 5d6 damage per 10 seconds in the form of blisters and burns.  At
the end of the duration, the boiling earth cools to its normal tempera-
ture, effectively trapping all affected within the area of effect.  Any
submerged beings will begin to asphyxiate.
Notes: Must be cast on soft ground such as earth or sand.  Has no
effect on hard ground such as stone.
Piety Level 17
SINKHOLE
Align Restrict Neutral 
Save:  N/A
Prayer Time:  85 minutes
Restrictions: 1 use /  5 PC
Casting Time: 10 seconds
Range:  3' per PC
Area Affected: 10' diameter circle Duration:  Instantaneous
Distance to Focus: 3 per PC
Description: Causes a pit the size of the area affected with a depth 
of 3' per PC of the invoker to form just underneath the top-most 
layer of the ground.  While the area appears to be firm and solid, 
any non-flying being within 3 of the center of the effect must make 
a 1/2 Agility save.   Similarly, any being within the remaining 
diameter must make an Agility save.  Success indicates the being was 
able to jump clear of the sinkhole.  Failure indicates the being has 
fallen to the depth of the sinkhole, taking appropriate falling 
damage.  Once the invocation has been triggered, the thin layer of 
ground covering the sinkhole collapses into the hole.  Likewise, the 
magic holding back the earthen walls ceases, causing the hole to be 
filled, effectively burying anyone trapped within.  Beings that are
buried alive begin to asphyxiate.  
Notes: Cannot be cast on a rocky surface.  If cast underwater, any 
beings within the diameter area of effect as well as any within a 
height equal to the PC of the invoker must make a 1/4 Agility save 
to avoid being forcefully sucked into the sinkhole, suffering the ill 
effects described above.
Piety Level 18
PROTECTION FROM NATURE
Alignment Restriction: Neutral Save: Control Resistance
Prayer Time: 90 minutes
Uses: 1 per 10 Piety Control 
Invoking Time: 10 seconds
Range: None
Area Affected: 1 Being/2 PC
Duration: 30 min/PC
Distance to Focus: 2 per Piety Control
Description: Protects one being per 2 Piety Control from being
attacked or targeted by spells, invocations and effects originating from
Animal, Magical Animal, Plant, Magical Plant and Slime creatures. 
Notes: Greater slimes, sapient and magical animals and sapient and
magical plants get a Control Resistance.  The invocation has no effect
on arch slimes.  Protection from Nature cannot be invoked on an
Animal, Magical Animal, Plant, Magical Plant or Slime creatures.
Piety Level 20
CLIMATE CHANGE 
Alignment Restrict Neutral
Save:  N/A
Prayer Time: 100 minutes
Uses: 1 use / 12 PC
Invoking Time: 1 Day
Range: Sight
Area Affected: See Below
Duration: 1 Season
Distance to Focus: 1 mile per PC
Description: Works as the invocation Control Weather but effects will
last for an entire season once the invocation is complete. It can be set to
a certain time pattern as well, making it rain for a few hours or making
it clear during certain hours regardless of the usual weather. Multiple
invokings can create exceptional or truly horrendous weather depending
on the invokers desire. 
Notes: There may be unintended consequences to other areas around
the changed climate. For instance, if it is made to rain every day for
several hours, then clouds may not form in other nearby regions.
Continuous rain would bring flooding and quickly destroy a region. It
would also require more and more of the natural condensation from
other regions to keep up the rain.
